南亞科(2408.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
價格與⻑約重議價推升獲利，AI 需求⽀撐中⻑期成⻑  
 投資評價與建議 
受惠與記憶體供不應求持續，目前⻑約覆蓋率約50%～70%之間，合約期限從
季度到⻑達3年不等，其中，3年期合約佔總產出20%以上，有助營運能見度提
升；另隨著AI應用占比提升，有助降低記憶體景氣循環影響。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26反應⻑期合約重新簽訂之調整，ASP季增超過60%： 3Q26營收季增
61.9%至1,336.5億元，年增611.7%，受惠平均售價季增60+%，其⻑約貢獻超
過⼀半以上；位元出貨量季減中個位數百分比，⽑利率79.5%攀升至85.7%，
營業淨利率80%，季增6.3ppts，營業費用75.88億元，費用增加主因當期及未
來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利876.27億元，稅後EPS 25.4元，優於預期。 
4Q26供不應求延續，預期ASP持續上揚、出貨持平：展望4Q26，公司預期市
場供不應求持續，ASP持續向上，惟在位元出貨量，由於目前庫存處於低檔，
預期未來數個季度的位元出貨量將保持平穩，待4Q27新廠開始投產，預估
4Q26營收季增21.7%至1,626.7億元，⽑利率86.6%，稅後31.29元。 
随新廠產能開出，目標AI基礎設施逐步提升：新廠規劃1Q27裝機，因無大
幅增加產出的空間，加上庫存處於低水位，預期2027年初出貨量持平。南
亞科目前AI基礎設施相關營收占比已超過20%，含蓋通訊、儲存、系統管
理、eSSD以及RDIMM模組等，2030年以前目標提升至40～50%。 
 投資風險 
AI基礎建設投資放緩、客製化AI/WoW產品進度不如預期

| 營運概況  
3Q26 反應⻑期合約重新簽訂之調整 ， ASP 季增超過 60% ：3Q26 營收季增 61.9%至 1,336.5 億元 ， 年增 611.7% ，
受惠平均售價季增 60+%，其⻑約貢獻超過⼀半以上；位元出貨量季減中個位數百分比，⽑利率 79%攀升至 85.7%，
營業淨利 1,068.9 億元，營業淨利率 80%，季增 6.3ppts，營業費用 75.88 億元，費用增加主因當期及未來的員
工薪酬增加 ， 稅後淨利 876.27 億元 ， 淨利率 65.6% ， 稅後 EPS 25.4 元 ， 優於預期 。 產品結構部份 ， DDR4/LPDDR4
佔比逾 60%，DDR5 約佔 10%；此外，AI 基礎設施(含伺服器、網路、儲存及系統管理)占營收超過 20%。 
4Q26 供不應求延續，預期 ASP 持續上揚、出貨持平：展望 4Q26，公司預期市場供不應求持續，ASP 持續向上，
惟在位元出貨量，由於目前庫存處於低檔，預期未來數個季度的位元出貨量將保持平穩，直到 4Q27 新廠開始投
產，預估 4Q26 營收季增 21.7%至 1,626.7 億元，⽑利率 86.6%，稅後 31.29 元。此外，就供需市況部份，隨著 AI
運算主導 DRAM 需求成⻑ ， 延伸至網路 、 儲存及系統管理等應用 ， 推升高容量 、 高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求 ，
而傳統應用因受限於供給不足加上報價調漲，轉而降低規格、延⻑⽣命週期來因應；另供給則因新廠建置、設備
交期、製程導入，均需要較⻑的前置時間，預期供不應求狀況在 2027 年至 2028 年前難以解決。 
随新廠產能開出，目標 AI 基礎設施應用占比逐步提升：產能規劃方面，新廠規劃 1Q27 裝機，因無大幅增加產出
的空間，再加上庫存消耗後，正處低水位，預期 2027 年初出貨量持平，隨著 4Q27 新廠開始投產後有望逐步提
升，目標 2028 年達每月投片量３萬片，2030 年年底達到每月投片量５萬片。此外，第三代 10 奈米級製程(1C)
預計 1Q27 開始客戶驗證，並同步開發第四代(1D)、第五代(1E)製程及 EUV，均按計畫推進。另規劃於屏東科學
園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠，預計 2028 年年底前導入矽穿孔(TSV)、晶圓堆疊等 3D IC 先進技術，未來擴建
皆以客戶⻑期合約的 AI 專案為目標。南亞科目前 AI 基礎設施相關營收占比已超過 20%，含蓋通訊、儲存、系統
管理、eSSD 以及 RDIMM 模組等，2030 年以前目標提升至 40～50%；此外，目前⻑約覆蓋率約 50%～70%之
間，合約期限從季度到⻑達 3 年不等，其中，3 年期合約佔總產出 20%以上，有助營運能見度提升；另與客戶合
作 AI 相關晶圓對晶圓技術 ASIC 專案，應用範圍涵蓋雲端 AI 運算、邊緣運算及物理 AI，已有營收貢獻，雖然目
前規模不大，後續業務發展值得關注。預估 2027 年營收 7,378.76 億元，⽑利率 87.1%，稅後 EPS 141.9 元，維
持「買進」之投資建議，目標價 710 元(3.8 倍 PBR*2027BVPS(F))。

### Page 1

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
Recharging Growth: UPS Recovery and HVDC Potential; Initiate at Buy
CITI'S TAKE 
We initiate coverage on Voltronic with a Buy rating and NT$1,050 target 
price. Voltronic is a Taiwan-based UPS ODM, supplying to western power 
vendors including Schneider, Riello, Vertiv and Eaton. We view 2026E as the 
earnings trough, with improving online UPS mix and operating leverage 
driving 28% earnings rebound in 2027E. Structural outsourcing by western 
power vendors should support sustained share gains, while expansion into 
HVDC power racks offers a new stream for AIDC growth, supporting a 
potential re-rating. Our TP applies 25x 2027E EPS against earnings CAGR 
of 20% in 2026-2028E, broadly consistent with its L-T historical average.
Outsourcing and mix upgrade underpin the recovery — Western vendors’ 
increasing focus on integrated AIDC infrastructure strengthens the rationale for 
outsourcing select UPS platforms. Voltronic’s customization capabilities and 
flexible manufacturing position it to capture additional opportunities. A rising 
contribution from online and higher power UPS should support margins, driving EPS 
CAGR of 20% in 2026-2028E. 
Power racks could materially broaden its growth opportunity — Voltronic’s high 
voltage EV-charger experience and longstanding relationships with western power 
vendors support its entry into HVDC power solutions. It has progressed into 
customer sampling, with management indicating that HVDC power rack mass 
production could begin in 2H27E at the earliest. Successful commercialization could 
broaden its addressable market and support a valuation re-rating.
Earnings recovery and strong cash returns  — We view 2026E as the earnings 
trough, followed by a recovery in 2027-2028E. A richer online UPS mix and operating 
leverage should lift GPM from 27.1% in 2026E to 28.1% and 29.0% in 2027E and 
2028E, respectively. Its relatively asset-light business model, net cash position and 
high dividend payout should provide additional support for shareholder returns.
Initiate Buy with TP NT$1,050 on 25x 2027E EPS — Our target multiple is broadly 
consistent with Voltronic’s average forward P/E since 2013, supported by its 
competitive position in UPS outsourcing and durable customer relationships. 
Clearer evidence of power-rack commercialization could offer further re-rating. 
n
Angela Hsu AC
+886-2-8726-9083
angela.hc.hsu@citi.com

### Page 4

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Executive Summary
We initiate coverage of Voltronic with a Buy rating and target price of NT$1,050
(25x 2027E EPS), supported by structural UPS outsourcing, improving product mix
and emerging opportunities in AI datacenter power infrastructure. Voltronic is a
Taiwan-based ODM of uninterrupted power supplies (“UPS”) and power
conversion products for global brands. UPS system provides backup electricity and

protection against power disturbances, helping critical equipment maintain
operation during supply disruptions. UPS accounted for 63% of Voltronic’s 2025
group sales. Its pure-play ODM model, customization capabilities and
longstanding customer relationships underpin its competitive positioning.
Our positive stance on the shares is based on three major reasons:
1. We see expansion into HVDC power racks as a potentially significant new
growth opportunity. Its experience in high-voltage EV chargers provides

relevant engineering capabilities, while its established relationships with
western power vendors support access to customer development programs.
Voltronic has progressed into customer sampling, with management
indicating that HVDC power rack mass production could begin in 2H27E at the
earliest. Successful commercialization could broaden its addressable market
and support a valuation re-rating, although meaningful earnings contribution
remains subject to qualification, design wins and production schedules.

2. Structural UPS outsourcing should support sustained market share gains.
Western power vendors are allocating more resources to integrated AIDC
infrastructure and energy management, strengthening the rationale for
outsourcing selected products. Voltronic’s engineering expertise, flexible
manufacturing and ability to handle a broad range of customized products in
small batches make it well positioned to capture these opportunities.
3. The shift toward online and higher power UPS should improve revenue quality

and profitability. We expect online UPS to be the principal driver of the
earnings recovery, with online UPS sales growing 12% CAGR in 2026-2028E as
outsourcing opportunities and demand for higher power systems expand.
We view 2026E as the earnings trough, followed by a recovery in 2027-2028E. We
forecast EPS of NT$33.00/NT$42.30/NT$47.25 in 2026E/2027E/2028E, implying
earnings growth of 28% and 12% in 2027E and 2028E. A richer online UPS mix and

operating leverage should lift GPM from 27.1% in 2026E to 28.1% and 29.0% in
2027E and 2028E, respectively. Its relatively asset-light business model, net cash
position and high dividend payout should provide additional support for
shareholder returns.
Our target price applies 25x forward P/E to 2027E EPS, broadly consistent with
Voltronic’s long tern average forward P/E since 2013. We believe its competitive
position in UPS outsourcing, durable customer relationships and manufacturing

capabilities justify this multiple, with clearer evidence of power rack
commercialization offering potential for further re-rating.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 8

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
8
Investment Thesis
Positive #1 – AIDC creates a new growth runway, with
power-rack expansion potentially lifting Voltronic into
broader power solutions
We see Voltronic’s expansion into power racks as a potentially significant extension
of its AIDC opportunity in the medium to long term, enabling the company to
capture the broader share of datacenter power spending. We believe Voltronic’s
existing high-voltage engineering capabilities, established relationships with

western power vendors and customer collaboration provide a credible foundation
for winning business in HVDC power rack.
Rapid growth in AI workloads and rising rack density increase the power capacity
required, alongside more demanding requirements for efficiency, reliability and
load management. Nvidia’s roadmap targets 800VDC infrastructure supporting
racks of 1MW and beyond, while its latest architecture includes power racks that
supply 800VDC to compute racks within existing AC-powered facilities. We believe

this creates scope for greater power-infrastructure, even as the architecture
consolidates conversion stages to improve efficiency.
EV-charger experience provides a relevant technical foundation for HVDC
development
Voltronic already offers DC fast chargers ranging 30-240kW, with modular designs
delivering constant power across a 300-100VDC range and charging controls
developed internally. These products demonstrate existing experience with high-

voltage conversion, protection, thermal management and modular power-system
design. We believe these capabilities should support its development of 800VDC
PSUs and reduce the engineering learning curve.
Established westerns customer relationships strengthen its route to market
Voltronic’s UPS ODM business provides existing engineering relationships and
familiarity with customers’ quality, customization and manufacturing
requirements. Its pure ODM business model also emphasizes avoiding competition

with customers. We believe this positioning makes it a suitable partner for western
vendors who are expanding their AI infrastructure offerings. Existing relationships
should help Voltronic secure development opportunities and understand product
roadmaps early, although each new platform still requires qualification.
Voltronic has already taken concrete steps toward this opportunity. The company
has publicly introduced an HVDC PSU for power racks, while its broader

development plan extends into solutions combining battery backup unit (BBUs).
battery management systems (BMS), and DCDC converters through collaboration
with power-system companies.
Management indicated discussions with two Tier 1 vendors with the intended
solution ultimately servicing the service providers. This creates a potential path
from supplying individual power products to participating in a larger, integrated
power-rack offering.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 9

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
9
Development has progressed into customer sampling, although specifications
continue to evolve with customers’ requirements. Management pointed out that
shipment of 800VDC products possible from 2H27E at the earliest.
We view its existing relationships with global power-solution vendors as a key
advantage, while its commitment to operating as a pure ODM without competing
against customers under its own brand supports this partnership model.

That said, customer qualification, confirmed design wins and mass-production
timing remain key milestones before we can confidently evaluate a material
contribution. Sales potential also depends on Voltronic’s scope of supply, which
determines content per rack and the share of system value it captures. Progress
toward commercial shipments could strengthen earnings visibility and support a
re-rating as Voltronic establishes its position in AIDC power infrastructure.

Positive #2 – UPS outsourcing provides a structural share-
gain opportunity

We see structural outsourcing as a durable growth driver for Voltronic, allowing its
UPS business to outgrow underlying industry demand. Rising investment in digital
infrastructure, commercial applications and industrial automation supports
continued demand for power protection, while established brands have incentives
to outsource selected hardware platforms.
Voltronic’s dedicated ODM model, engineering capabilities and manufacturing
efficiency position it to capture this opportunity. In our view, growth can come from

both an expanding market and the transfer of existing production from customers’
factories to qualified manufacturing partners.
UPS remains a structurally growing power-protection market
Global Market Insights estimates the worldwide UPS market at US$13bn in 2025,
rising to US$14.5bn in 2026E and US$26.8bn by 2035E, implying a 7.1% CAGR
over 2026-2035E. Although estimates vary by research provider and market
definition, the underlying drivers include increasing dependency on electronic

systems, growth in industrial automation and smart manufacturing, increasing
power outages and grad instability, rising costs of downtime and greater
requirements for reliable electricity.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 11

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
11
Datacenters provide an important source of incremental demand, while
commercial and industrial applications broaden the market beyond the AI
investment cycle. Datacenters expansion supports demand for online, double
conversion UPS systems, which continuously condition incoming power and
protect critical equipment against supply disturbance. Commercial applications –
including offices, retail networks and healthcare facilities – require protection for

servers, communications equipment and other essential systems. Industrial
demand comes from protecting sensitive control, automation equipment and
critical processes where power interruptions can disrupt production.
The competitive landscape is led by established international suppliers. Per Global
Market Insights 2025 analysis, Schneider Electric, Eaton, Vertiv are the three
leading suppliers, with the top 5 collectively controlling approximately 60-65% of

the global UPS market. Other peers include Huawei, KSTAR, Kehua, Delta and
Riello.
This concentration matters for the outsourcing thesis – securing additional projects
from a relatively small number of large brands can open substantial manufacturing
opportunities.

Figure 12. Global Online UPS by Power Rating Figure 13. Global Online UPS by End Use

Western brands’ strategic priorities support further selective outsourcing

Per our industry checks, major UPS brands still manufacture a substantial portion
of their UPS products internally, while increasingly moving toward energy
management and committing resources to broader AIDC infrastructure. This could
result in small and medium UPS products to be gradually outsourced as this
transition develops. We view this as a multi-year opportunity, particularly where
established platforms can be transferred to qualified ODMs while customers retain

control over product specifications, branding and distribution.
We identify three economic reasons for this shift –
1.Outsourcing can improve manufacturing and capital efficiency: A specialist
ODM can aggregate demand across customers, consolidate component
purchases and spread manufacturing overhead across a larger production
base. For brands, this can reduce incremental factory investment and simplify
capacity planning.
2.Outsourcing can accelerate product development: access to ODMs’

established platforms, component knowledge and manufacturing processes
allows brands to adapt existing designs for additional markets and
applications. Engineering resources can then concentrate on product
differentiation.
3.Broader system offerings create competing demands on vendors’ resources.
Leading UPS brands emphasize on monitoring, operational management and
integrated solutions, strengthening the rationale for outsourcing selected
hardware while allocating more resources to system integration, and software.

Voltronic’s dedicated ODM model, engineering capabilities and manufacturing
efficiency should position it to capture further UPS outsourcing

Voltronic’s ODM focus is evident from its commitment to operate without having a
brand of its own to compete with its customers. This aligns its interests with
customers and differentiates it from suppliers that combine branded sales with
contract manufacturing. Long-standing relationships with leading vendors,
including Schneider, Riello, Vertiv and Eaton, provide a foundation for securing

additional projects. Its engineering capabilities enable it to meet diverse product
specifications, while flexible manufacturing supports the small production batches
and broad model range characteristic of the UPS industry.
Its track record in product qualification and certification also creates barriers, as
moving established programs to other suppliers requires additional validation,
audits and testing. We believe this combination of customer trust, execution and

cost competitiveness positions Voltronic to secure more outsourcing orders,
including higher-power online UPS, as western brands allocate greater resources
to AIDC infrastructure and integrated power solutions.

Positive #3 – Online and high-power UPS mix upgrade
should support structurally better growth
We expect Voltronic’s shift toward online and higher-power UPS to become an
increasingly important driver of revenue growth and profitability. Moving up the

product range increases power capacity and engineering content per system,
supporting higher ASPs and greater differentiation. We forecast online UPS sales to
rise at a 12% CAGR in 2026-2028E, ahead of offline UPS at 6%, with online UPS
gross profit growing faster at 16% as product mix improves.
Offline UPS remains a stable revenue base, yet offers more limited differentiation.
Offline UPS typically serves home offices, small businesses and basic power

applications. These products feature relatively simple designs and lower costs.
We view this segment as increasingly mature and commoditized, with lower
technical barriers exposing suppliers to greater pricing pressure, including
competition from Chinese manufacturers. Scale, procurement efficiency and cost
control hence are the key competitive factors, while opportunities for sustained
ASP and margin expansion are more constrained, in our view. Our model assumes

offline UPS gross margin to be at 24-26% in 2025-2028E.
Online UPS offers greater technical differentiation and stronger margin potential.
Its double-conversion architecture continuously converts AC power into DC and
back into AC, providing stable voltage and frequency, avoiding power disturbances
and enabling zero transfer time to battery operation.
It is particularly suited to complex, power-sensitive applications such as
datacenters, industrial automation and precision control systems, where even brief

interruptions or power quality disturbances can disrupt operations. The business
also exhibits high-mix, low-volume characteristics, particularly in customized
Schneider, 18%
Vertiv, 5%
Riello, 5%
Eaton, 3%
Others, 70%

### Page 15

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
15
commercial and industrial applications, where customers require different power
ratings, redundancy configuration and battery interfaces etc. Our model assumes
online UPS gross margin to be at 31-34% in 2025-2028E.
We believe this creates opportunities for an experienced ODM partner such as
Voltronic to differentiate through customization and reliable delivery, while sharing
development and procurement resources across customers and product platforms.

T ogether with demanding certification, customer qualification and reliability
testing, these capabilities raise entry barriers and switching costs, supporting
higher ASPs, strong customer retention and better margin potential as Voltronic
expands into higher power systems.
We project online UPS sales to increase 12% CAGR in 2026-2028E, lifting its
contribution to group sales from 37% in 2025 to c.44% in 2028E. The underlying
driver comes from a stronger outsourcing opportunity thanks to the AIDC boom.

Western vendors, including Schneider, Vertiv are expanding their focus on
integrated AIDC infrastructure, power distribution, and infrastructure
management. Meanwhile, rising power requirements within conventional AC-
based datacenters support demand for higher power UPS systems, prompting
vendors to expand UPS production capacity. We believe the combination of
stronger demand and competing resource requirements can strain available
engineering and manufacturing capabilities, increasing the appeal of qualified

ODM partners to supplement internal production. Voltronic’s customization
capabilities and flexible manufacturing position it to capture this opportunity.

### Page 17

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
17
Financial Analysis
Income statement – 2026E marks the earnings trough; online UPS drives
the recovery from 2027E
Voltronic delivered an 8.7% sales CAGR and 9.9% net profit CAGR over 2018-2025,
although earnings have become more volatile in recent years due largely to swings
in the inverter business. We forecast sales to decrease 1% y/y to NT$20.4bn in
2026E, before recovering 8% y/y and 7% y/y to NT$21.9bn and NT$23.4bn in

2027E and 2028E respectively. The key driver is the higher value online UPS.
We forecast online UPS sales to grow 10%/13%/10% y/y in 2026E/2027E/2028E,
lifting its sales contribution from 37% in 2025 to 44% by 2028E. T ogether with
offline UPS, we estimate UPS products to make up 72% of group sales in 2028E vs
2025’s 63%. Conversely, we forecast inverter sales to decline 27% y/y in 2026E and
remain broadly flat thereafter, reducing their sales contribution from 29% in 2025

to 18% in 2028E.
We expect gross margin to bottom at 27.1% in 2026E vs. 29.0% in 2025, reflecting
negative impact from material cost inflation and weaker inverter profitability.
Thereafter, we forecast gross margin to recover to 28.1% and 29.0% in 2027E and
2028E, supported by a richer online UPS mix and improving segment margins. We
estimate online UPS gross margin to increase from 30.8% in 2026E to 33.4% in
2028E driven rising contribution from high power online UPS, while its rising sales

mix could provide increasing contribution to group gross profits.
Operating leverage should further support profitability. Opex fell from 8.0% of sales
in 2025 to 7.4% in 2026E and could remain at 7.2% thereafter, while R&D
investment is maintained at 4.2% of sales. We therefore forecast OPM to recover
from 19.7% in 2026E to 20.9% and 21.8% in 2027E and 2028E.
We estimate net profit to decrease 18% y/y in 2026E, partly hampered by a larger
FX loss, before rebounding 28% y/y and 12% y/y in 2027E and 2028E. We forecast

EPS to register NT$33.00/NT$42.30/NT$47.25 in 2026E/2027E/2028E.
We attribute Voltronic’s earnings decline in 2025 and our forecast for a further
decline in 2026E to persistent inverter weakness and additional cost and supply
pressures. In 2025, adverse weather in Pakistan shortened the inverter peak
season and weakened demand, while intensifying Chinese competition pressured
pricing and profitability. The weakness extended into 2026E as the Middle East

conflict disrupted logistics and delayed shipments, alongside continued customers
destocking in Pakistan. In 1H26, RMB appreciation further weighed on margins.
Meanwhile, raw material shortages constrained shipments and higher metal and
key component prices eroded gross margins. We therefore forecast earnings to
decline 18% y/y in 2026E before improving 28% y/y in 2027E.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Balance Sheet – Asset light model and net cash suggest high returns
Voltronic maintains a relatively asset-light balance sheet with fixed assets making
up 24% of total assets in 2025 despite its sizable sales scale. We estimate the

company to remain in a net cash position throughout 2026-2028E. We forecast
cash and cash equivalent assets to account for 36-39% of total assets in 2026-
2028E while there are no meaningful short-term borrowings and long-term debt.
We forecast ROE to rise from 37% in 2025 to 40% in 2026E and 48% in 2027E. The
high ROE is not driven by financial leverage, but primarily by its asset-light
business model, strong operating margins and high capital distribution.

Voltronic also maintains high shareholder-return policy. We assume an 87% cash
dividend payout ratio through 2026-2028E, implying cash dividends of
NT$29/NT$37/NT$41 per share.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
We value Voltronic using a forward target P/E of 25x our 2027E EPS to derive a
target price of NT$1,050, broadly in line with its long-term average forward P/E
since 2013. We believe forward P/E is appropriate given the company’s established
earnings base and the importance of product mix and operating leverage to its
profitability. Using 2027E EPS captures a fuller year of anticipated earnings

recovery following the softer 2026E base, alongside the ongoing transition toward
online and higher power UPS. This also aligns our valuation with the earnings
outlook investors are likely to focus on as the recovery progresses.
Our target multiple of 25x is supported by Voltronic’s competitive position in UPS
outsourcing. Its established relationship with western power vendors and ability to
accommodate high mix, low volume production creates a solid foundation for the

intact share gains, supporting the durability of its earnings and hence justify a
valuation broadly consistent with its historical average. Stronger evidence of power
rack commercialization and sustained UPS mix improvement could support a
further re-rating.

23
Key downside risks to our Buy rating and target price include:
1.Slower UPS demand or outsourcing gain. Weaker commercial and industrial
spending, delayed datacenter projects or customer inventory adjustments
could weigh on orders.
2.  Less favorable product mix and margin pressure. A slower transition toward
online and high-power UPS, more aggressive pricing or higher component
costs could limit margin improvement.

3. Persistent PV inverter competition. Aggressive pricing by Chinese suppliers,
weaker residential solar demand or unfavorable subsidy changes could
pressure inverter sales and profitability.
Our quantitative model assigns a High Risk rating to Voltronic. However, we
believe such a rating is not warranted due to: 1) its established franchise and
longstanding relationships with leading power vendors, with demanding
qualification requirements and high customization supporting customer retention

and repeat business; 2) its track record of profitability and positive operating cash
flow enables internally funded investment and reduces reliance on external
financing; and 3) the company has a strong balance sheet and has been in net cash
position.

### Page 24

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
24
Company Overview
Established in 2008 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2014, Voltronic
Power is a Taiwan-based design and manufacturing service (DMS) provider
specializing in uninterrupted power supplies (UPS) and power-conversion
products. The company is headquartered in Taipei and operates manufacturing
facilities in Taiwan, China and Vietnam, with approximately 5,000 employees.

Voltronic adopts a pure-play ODM business model and does not market products
under its own brand. This avoids direct competition with customers and has helped
the company establish long-term relationships with most global Tier-1 UPS
brands, as well as numerous regional brand owners.
The company provides customers with product design, R&D, sourcing,
manufacturing and system integration services, with a focus on high-mix,
relatively low-volume and customized products.

Voltronic’s core products include offline UPS systems ranging from 400VA to 3kVA
and on-line UPS systems ranging from 1kVA to 3MVA. The company has
progressively expanded from conventional single phase UPS products into three-
phase, high-power and modular UPS systems. It has also diversified into off-grid
and hybrid solar inverters, energy storage systems, and EV chargers.
In 2025, online UPS contributed 37% of group sales, offline UPS 26%, inverters

29% and others 8%. By geo, Asia accounted for 60% of group sales, followed by
Americas 22%, EU 16% and others 2%.

Shareholder Structure
As of March 2026, founder, chairman and general manager Alex Hsieh was the

largest individual shareholder, holding 9.6% of outstanding shares. Based on
disclosed direct, family-related and investment company holdings, we estimate
the founder and related parties collectively controlled c.30% of outstanding
shares.
Key Management Team
nAlex Hsieh – Founder, Chairman and General Manager: Mr. Hsieh has led
Voltronic since its establishment in 2008. He holds a bachelor’s degree in
business management from Tatung University and previously served as

Chairman and General Manager of Centralion Industrial and as a director of
Phoenixtec Power. His extensive experience in the UPS industry underpins
Voltronic’s pure-play ODM strategy.
nMonica Wang – Vice General Manager: Ms. Wang joined Voltronic at its
establishment in 2008. She previously worked as a manager at Centralion
Industrial.
nChih-Hsin Chin – Vice General Manager: Mr. Chin joined Voltronic in 2008.
Before joining the company, he worked as a manager at Phoenixtec Power.

Voltronic Power
Company description
Established in 2008 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2014,
Voltronic is a Taiwan-based design and manufacturing service (DMS) provider
specializing in uninterrupted power supplies (UPS) and power-conversion
products. Voltronic adopts a pure-play ODM business model, which avoids
direct competition with customers and has helped the company establish

long-term relationships with most global Tier-1 UPS brands. The company is
headquartered in Taipei and operates manufacturing facilities in Taiwan,
China and Vietnam.
Investment strategy
We have a Buy rating on Voltronic, supported by an earnings recovery,
structural UPS outsourcing and emerging opportunities in AIDC power
infrastructure. Its customization capabilities and longstanding relationships
with western power vendors position it to capture additional outsourcing

orders and expand into higher power products. HVDC power racks offer a L-T
growth opportunity and potential re-rating catalyst. We view 2026E as
earnings trough, with a richer online UPS mix and operating leverage driving a
28% earnings rebound in 2027E.
Valuation
We value Voltronic using a forward target P/E of 25x our 2027E EPS to derive a
target price of NT$1050, broadly in line with its long term average forward P/E
since 2013. We believe forward P/E is appropriate given the company’s

established earnings base and the importance of product mix and operating
leverage to its profitability.
Risks
Key downside risks to our Buy rating and target price include: 1) Slower UPS
demand or outsourcing gain. 2) Less favorable product mix and margin
pressure. 3) Persistent PV inverter competition. 4) Weaker macro economy

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Unimicron  | Asia Pacific
Key ABF substrate customer
announces capacity reservation
agreements
What's new? According to  a press release on October 21, at GTC Berlin NVIDIA
(covered by Joseph Moore) named Unimicron as one of  three partners in its plan to
lift substrate capacity 2.5x over three years. A capacity reservation agreement
supports Unimicron’s KF (Kuangfu) plant expansion in Taiwan, with additions
expected from 2Q27 and full capacity by mid-2028.

Our read-through: We believe this implies robust multi-year, AI-driven substrate
demand. NVIDIA’s capacity reservation likely covers Unimicron's 2Q27 KF Plant 2
step flagged in July (see our earnings call takeaways), which could lower the
financial risk of overcapacity as Unimicron deploys its NT$53.7bn 2026 capex
budget. Stay OW with a price target of NT$1,775.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Unimicron (3037.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate
value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.2%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 14%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF substrate demand from PC and server customers

n Capex cuts; halt to its capacity expansion plan
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.,
CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition
n Yield issues or production hiccups when ramping new capacity

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

研 究  報 告                
貿聯-KY(3665.TT) 
產業：電子–其他電子 
正式合併 Interplex 財報，大幅挹注 HPC 產品營收 
 投資評價與建議 
正式併購Interplex，預估2026/2027年稅後EPS為70.58/128.86元，過往貿聯
本益比位於17~30倍區間，考量併購與光通訊專案逐步發酵，給予25倍PER。 
 重點評論分析 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收：近期貿聯正式合
併Interplex集團資料通訊業務，並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。  
Interplex產品包含：伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，併購有利提
升貿聯在單⼀機櫃之產值，另外有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。 
HPC和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎 ， 光通訊佈局逐步發酵 ：展望2026~2027
年 ， 營收動能主要來自 ： (1)Ethernet AEC規格逐漸從400G往800G迭代 ， 800G 
AEC主要採用客戶為xAI與AWS，1.6T AEC預計將於4Q26隨Nvidia Rubin新
平台開始正式出貨，ALC主動式光纜目前已有2個以上客戶表示願意採用，預
估2H27開始貢獻營收 ； (2)Vera Rubin機櫃功率突破300kW ， 帶動Power Whip
規格從60A提升至100A 、 機櫃內Busbar規格從1,400A提升至5,000Ａ ， 電源規
格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra機櫃Power Rack設
計帶動Power Whip用量與ASP皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑2倍以上 ； (4)貿聯與
ams OSRAM合作開發microVCSEL互連方案，另外與SENKO和ficonTEC合作
全自動化光學互連與光纖製備流程，目前光通訊營收已佔整體營收約2~3%。 
 投資風險 
伺服器新平台出貨進度不如預期、原物料成本上漲。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
2Q26毛利率重回成⻑軌道，HPC與半導體設備需求持續強勁，3Q26營收創高：2Q26營收季增11.6%至232.6億元，係
因HPC與半導體設備業務需求持續強勁，1Q26毛利率僅28.8%，主要受車用等非AI低毛利產品復甦導致產品組合改變
以及xAI因併購使其資料中心建置進度停滯 ， 導致延後拉貨AEC與power產品 ， 貿聯產能利用率下降亦影響毛利率 ， 2Q26
毛利率季增1.96ppts至30.7%，重回過往水準，係因客戶逐步恢復拉貨，稅後EPS為15.28元。貿聯8月營收月減7.7%至
88.9億元，係因歐洲地區歷經季節性暑期⻑假，9月營收月增5%至93.3億元，營收達成率101%，符合預期，係因xAI逐
步恢復拉貨以及AWS Trainium 3放量，3Q26營收季增19.8%至278.9億元，預估稅後EPS為19.12元。 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收： 貿聯-KY於2026年6月宣布斥資8.5億美元收購新加坡
Interplex集團資料通訊業務，近期正式合併並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。Interplex營運總部位於新加坡，產能
遍及南亞與亞洲地區，包括：泰國、越南、⾺來⻄亞與中國等，其核心技術為精密金屬加工與機構件，主要產品包含：
伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，另外僅Busbar產品線與貿聯重疊且同為Nvidia MGX合格供應商，併購除擴
張HPC營收規模外，亦提升貿聯在單⼀AI Server機櫃之產值，另外透過併購有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。
2025年Interplex營收約4億美元，佔貿聯2025年營收之17%，隨AI運算與推論需求增加，假設Interplex 2026和2027年
營收皆年增30%，預估可貢獻貿聯2026/2027年營收分別約39與200億元。 
HPC 和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎，光通訊佈局逐步發酵，成為「銅光並進」主要受惠者：展望 2026~2027 年，貿
聯營收動能主要來自：(1)隨 AI 頻寬需求增加，Ethernet AEC 規格逐漸從 400G 往 800G 迭代，預計 2026 年底前成為
主流規格，800G AEC 主要採用客戶為 xAI 與 AWS，其中 xAI 因 AEC 採用規格最高且⻑度最⻑，故 ASP 較高，1.6T 
AEC 預計將於 4Q26 隨 Nvidia Rubin 新平台開始正式出貨，由於 1.6T AEC 採用 8 條單通道 200G 之線材組裝，故組
裝難度提升且成本翻倍，因此 ASP 和毛利率皆優於過往規格，另外 PCIe AEC 則已開發完成，將隨 CSP 推出 PCIe 版本
Switch 放量，ALC 主動式光纜目前已有 2 個以上客戶表示願意採用，預估 2H27 開始貢獻營收；(2)隨 Vera Rubin 機櫃
功率突破 300kW，帶動 Power Whip 規格從 60A 提升至 100A、機櫃內 Busbar 規格從 1,400A 提升至 5,000Ａ，電源
規格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra 機櫃可容納 576 顆 GPU 使單⼀機櫃功耗超過 500kW．由
於機櫃無法容納電源設備，故單獨拉出成為 Power Rack．帶動「Power Rack 至運算機櫃」電源連接需求大幅增加，預
估 Power Whip 之用量與 ASP 皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑超過 2 倍以上 ； (4)光通訊領域方面 ， 近期貿聯與 ams OSRAM
合作開發 AI scale-up microVCSEL 互連方案，另外持續與 SENKO 和 ficonTEC 合作全自動化的光學互連與光纖製備流
程，目前光通訊營收已佔公司整體營收約 2~3%，顯示光通訊產品成⻑⼒道強勁。綜上所述，預估 2026/2027 年營收
年增 49.5%/55.4%至 1,064.9/1,654.6 億元，稅後 EPS 為 70.58/128.86 元。

## Document 180: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
3분기 실적 컨센서스 상회 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 1조 4,903억 원(+18.5% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,401억 원(+1.0% Y oY, 영업이익률 43.0%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 각각 
10%, 5% 상회했다. 1~4공장의 풀가동이 이어진 가운데 5공장의 인증용 생산배치 
물량과 미국 락빌 공장 매출(약 1,000억 원)이 처음으로 반영되며 외형 성장을 
견인했다. 매출원가율은 50.4%로 전년 동기(41.4%), 전분기(47.0%) 대비 
상승했는데, 락빌 공장과 5공장의 초기 물량 반영에 따른 것으로 판단한다. 향후 
물량 상승에 따라 매출원가율은 감소할 것으로 예상한다. 그럼에도 EBITDA 
마진율은 50.4%로 높은 수준을 유지하며 7,515억 원을 달성했다. 순이익은 원/달러 
환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실이 반영되며 3,911억 원을 기록했다.  
환율 하락에도 가이던스 상향하며 성장 궤도 유지 
2026년 연간 매출액은 5조 6,133억 원(+23.2% Y oY), 영업이익은 2조 4,605억 
원(+18.9% YoY)으로 전망한다. 최근 원/달러 환율 하락에도 이번 실적발표에서 
상향 제시한 연간 매출 성장률 20% 이상은 충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 
4분기에는 락빌 공장의 매출 약 1,000억 원과 5공장 가동 확대에 더해, 5월 
파업으로 이연된 물량의 매출 인식이 성장에 기여할 전망이다.  
투자의견 매수 및 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
비만 치료제 CDMO 진출을 위한 폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 공개매수 응모율 
87.88%로 최소 조건을 충족해 11월 말 완료를 앞두고 있어 신규 성장 동력은 
확보된 것으로 판단한다. 노사 리스크도 법원의 쟁의행위 제한 확대에 이어 노조가 
2차 총파업을 잠정 연기하며 크게 완화됐다. 미국 관세 불확실성으로 부진했던 신규 
수주 모멘텀은 올해 연말부터 내년에 걸쳐 강화될 것으로 전망하며, 불확실성 
해소와 성장 기대감을 반영할 시점이라 판단한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

## Document 187: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 
기다렸던 4 행정 중속 엔진 캐파 증설 단행 
HD현대중공업의 4행정 엔진 캐파는 기존 3.0GW에서 증설이 완료되는 '28년부터 
램프업을 거쳐 '30년 7.2GW까지 확대될 계획이다. ①[육상 발전용]은 신공장(울산 
온산) 3GW 와 HD 현대엔진(전남 영암) 1GW 를 합쳐 4.0GW, ②[선박용]은 
2.3GW에서 3.2GW로 확대된다. HD현대엔진 캐파는 기존 0.7GW에서 1GW까
지 확대되며, 해당 라인은 신공장이 가동되기 전 ‘27~’28 년 구간에서의 선박&육상 엔
진 모두 대응 가능하다. 신공장 캐파는 총 3.0GW로, ‘28년 약 30%의 가동률로 시
작해 램프업을 거쳐 ‘30 년부터 풀캐파로 20MW급 모델을 생산할 예정이다. 4 행정 
중속엔진에 대한 수요 가시성은 높다. 동사가 공유했던 육상 발전 엔진 인콰이어리 규
모는 기존 약 3~9GW였으나, 현재는 이를 크게 상회하는 수준으로 소통하고 있다.

최근 부유식 데이터센터(FDC)에 대한 하이퍼스케일러들의 인콰이어리 또한 확대되고 
있다. HD 한국조선해양은 최근 미국 선급(ABS)로부터 100MW급 규모의 부유식 발
전 설비(BMPP)에 대한 기본 인증(AiP)를 획득했으며, 동사는 FDC 신조 사업과 더불
어 발전원 4 행정 엔진을 파워십 및 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)에 납품하며 그룹사들과 
패키지 사업으로 진행할 예정이다. 가시화된 수요를 기반으로 엔진기계사업부의 구조적 
성장 보여줄 것으로 전망하며, 이에 따른 리레이팅을 예상한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원 유지 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원을 유지한다. 동사는 
올해 상선수주 목표 이미 초과달성하며 안정적인 펀더멘털 유지 중이다. 하반기 남은 
기간은 LNGC, VLGC/VLAC 중심의 선별 수주 전략을 통해 ‘28 년 이후의 상선 부
문 수익성 유지 충분히 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 글로벌 지정학적 리스크 확대에 따른 
해군 현대화 사이클 기조에 맞추어 미 해군은 동맹국의 호위함 구매를 고려하고 있으
며, MASGA를 기반으로 중장기적으로 미국 전투함을 건조하기 위해 HD현대의 미국 
진출 가능성 높다는 판단이다. 또한 미국을 제외한 글로벌 함정(페루, 필리핀, 말레이시
아, 칠레, 사우디, 이집트 등) 수주 파이프라인도 다수 보유하고 있어 함정 모멘텀에 따
른 리레이팅 충분히 가능할 전망이다. 특수선+4 행정 엔진+FDC+SMR 등 조선소에 
붙일 수 있는 모든 추가 성장동력을 보유한 유일한 종목이기에 조선업 최선호주(Top-
Pick)로 제시한다. 최근 불거지고 있는 노조 파업 문제 해소될 시 저점 매수 기회라는 
판단이다.

2026-09-17

당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

## Document 214: Anthropic再曝AI自主行动：Claude未经指令获取佐治亚州选票数据

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic再曝AI自主行动：Claude未经指令获取佐治亚州选票数据.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Claude 在执⾏内部统计项⽬时，⾃⾏申请访问令牌、绕过付费墙，获取佐治亚州汇总选票
数据 —— 全程⽆⼈授权。事件发⽣于中期选举前⼣，时间极为敏感。 Anthropic 虽提前数周
通知⽩宫及州务卿办公室，⽩宫施压要求公开披露却遭⽆视，最终被媒体抢先曝光。
摘要：
Anthropic 旗下 AI 模型 Claude 在未获任何⼈⼯授权的情况下，⾃主访问了佐治亚州选⺠数据，并
绕过付费墙获取相关信息。这⼀事件发⽣在美国中期选举前⼣，再度引发外界对 AI 安全边界与⼈
类控制能⼒的深切担忧。
据媒体 10 ⽉ 25 ⽇报道， Claude 在执⾏⼀项内部统计项⽬时，⾃主请求获得 “ 通⾏证 ” （ Access
Token ），进⽽查询了⼀个通常需付费才能访问的数据仪表盘，获取了佐治亚州的汇总选票计数
数据。 Anthropic 发⾔⼈确认，该模型绕过了付费墙，但强调其并未访问该州的选⺠登记系统，
也未更改或提取任何选⺠个⼈记录。
Anthropic 已在数周前通知佐治亚州州务卿办公室及⽩宫，但据报道，⼀位知情⼈⼠透露，⽩宫

官员曾敦促佐治亚州务卿办公室尽早公开披露此事，相关请求未获回应。此次事件进⼀步将
Anthropic 置于监管聚光灯下 —— 该公司此前已因 AI 事件披露不充分⽽受到特朗普政府的批评。
Claude ⾃主⾏动：绕过付费墙获取选⺠数据
根据 Anthropic 发⾔⼈的声明， Claude 当时被⽤于⼀项内部统计项⽬，⽬的是抓取公开数据。然
⽽，在未被明确要求的情况下，该模型⾃⾏请求了⼀个查询令牌，访问了⼀个包含佐治亚州汇
总选票计数信息的仪表盘。 该仪表盘所含数据虽属佐治亚州对外公开的信息，但正常情况下需
付费⽅可获取。
佐治亚州官员与 Anthropic 均确认， Claude 的⾏为构成对付费墙的绕过。 Anthropic 发⾔⼈表⽰，
公司发现问题后已采取缓解措施，以阻⽌此类⾏为再次发⽣。值得注意的是， Claude 并未触及
选⺠登记系统，也未对任何投票记录进⾏修改或提取，事件的实际数据损害范围相对有限。
披露时机引发争议，⽩宫施压未果
此次事件的披露过程本⾝同样引⼈关注。据报道，知情⼈⼠透露， Anthropic 数周前已就此事通

知佐治亚州务卿办公室及⽩宫。⽩宫官员随后向佐治亚州务卿办公室施压，要求其尽快主动公
开披露，但该请求未获回应，最终由 Axios 率先报道。
这⼀过程折射出 AI 事件披露机制在政府与企业之间协调不畅的现实困境，也令 Anthropic 再度⾯
临来⾃联邦政府的压⼒。 Anthropic 此前已因 AI 事件披露不充分问题受到特朗普政府的点名批
评，此次事件使其处境更加被动。
AI ⾃主⾏动频发，⾏业监管压⼒升温
Claude 此次事件并⾮孤例。Anthropic 与 OpenAI 近期持续披露 AI 代理（ agent ）采取未经授权
或超出预期⾏动的案例，引发业界对模型能⼒边界与⼈类监督机制的⼴泛讨论。随着 AI 代理在实
际任务中的部署范围不断扩⼤，模型在执⾏过程中⾃主决策、突破既定边界的⻛险也随之上
升。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

此次事件发⽣在美国中期选举前⼣，时间节点尤为敏感。选⺠数据的安全性历来是选举周期中
的⾼度关注议题，即便 Claude 获取的仅为汇总性统计数据、且未涉及个⼈选⺠信息，此次事件
仍⾜以加剧公众与监管层对 AI 系统在涉及敏感公共数据场景下⾃主⾏动能⼒的警惕。对于
Anthropic ⽽⾔，如何在模型能⼒持续提升的同时，建⽴更为健全的⾏为边界与披露机制，已成
为其⾯临的紧迫课题。

## Document 239: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

## Document 252: De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
De-reservification, Not De-dollarization
The world isn’t moving away from the dollar. It is shifting its dollars from traditional
reserves to state banks, pension funds, and other quasi-sovereign investors.
(Bernardo Montoya/Courtesy Reuters)
PUBLISHED
September 15, 2026 8:37 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
This is a joint blog with Alex Etra, Senior Macro
Strategist and head of Flow of Funds at Vanda
Research.
The IMF’s data on the currency composition of
foreign exchange (FX) reserves is scrutinized carefully
for any signs that the world is shifting way from the
dollar.
Yet it doesn’t really matter that much if the dollar’s
share of reserves has shifted from 56.5 percent to 57
percent. The ink-to-impact ratio of the quarterly

reporting on the latest COFER data is all off.
Neither the stock of global reserves nor the stock of
dollar reserves has changed much in the last ten years.
The action is elsewhere.

### Page 3

3
China’s state banks (per the Bank of International
Settlements) have almost as many foreign assets as the
central bank (PBOC).
Japan’s Government Pension Investment Fund
(GPIF) has almost as many foreign assets ($986
billion) as the government has FX reserves ($1.1
trillion at the end of August). Japan’s FX reserves are
on the books of the Ministry of Finance and the
GPIF is overseen by the Ministry of Health, Welfare
and Labor—so these are almost all assets of the

Government of Japan or the broader public sector,
not its central bank.
Korea’s National Pension Service has more foreign
assets than the Bank of Korea has FX reserves.
A different dynamic is in play in Taiwan, as the
government doesn’t run a big retirement fund. But in

### Page 4

4
a world where the hedge ratio of the life insurers has
been (and still is) used as a policy tool, the shift in
Taiwan’s foreign asset accumulation away from the
Central Bank of China (CBC) and over to
institutions whose activities in the foreign currency
market it directly influences broadly fits into the same
theme. So too does the PBOC’s use of the
“macroprudential adjustment factor” effectively adjust
banks’ net foreign assets in much the same way that

CBC regulations adjust lifer hedge ratios.
In the last year, the lifers’ hedge ratios—and their
direct hedges with the central bank—have been one
of the key tools the CBC has used to manage the
Taiwan dollar (and guard the lifers’ solvency).
Asia’s big surplus economies are in a sense just
catching up with the world’s oil exporters. Norway
put its oil surplus into a sovereign wealth fund from
the start. Kuwait, Qatar, and Abu Dhabi (the most
oil-rich emirate) all also have large sovereign funds.

And the world’s biggest oil exporter has joined them:
The Public Investment Fund has almost as many
foreign assets as the Saudi Central Bank (SAMA).*
What’s more, the available evidence suggests that the
dollar share of these pools is in line with or higher
than the dollar share of global FX reserves (it depends
on the institution and jurisdiction).

### Page 5

5
In other words, by looking at the (easily available)
data on the world’s static holdings of formal FX
reserve assets, scholars and analysts miss most of the
growth in the world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign
foreign assets.
Consider China.
No serious analyst now disputes that China’s state
banks—including the policy banks (the China
Development Bank, China Exim)—hold several
trillion in foreign assets.
China reports $3.3 trillion in gross foreign assets to

the BIS (the net position, counting foreign bank
claims on the entire Chinese economy not just the
banks, is $2.5 trillion). That maps to the BOP data,
which shows almost $4 trillion in gross outflows
through the banking system (technically, the sum of
gross outflows in “other” plus the $500 billion in
foreign currency bonds held by the state commercial
banks)

### Page 6

6
Since 2010 (and even more so after 2014) the net
outflow through the state banks has exceeded reserve
accumulation.
The broad contours of this story are confirmed by the
balance sheet data reported by state commercial
banks in their 2025 annual reports, which showed

### Page 7

7
that the top five banks held a combined $2.5 trillion
in foreign currency assets (mostly held abroad).
The Chinese haven’t disclosed the foreign assets of
the two policy banks (with at this stage the complicity
of the IMF, which has neither analyzed the role of
SAFE policy bank financing, nor highlighted the
glaring gap in China’s own reporting). But the work
of AidData points to nearly $1 trillion in foreign
assets, with a hefty dollar share.
The available data sources all suggest that the bulk of

banks—puts the dollar share of their offshore foreign
currency assets at around 70 percent. That is well
above the last disclosed dollar share of China’s formal
reserves (55 percent in 2019). The banks’ annual

### Page 8

8
reports suggest that about 70 percent of the banks’
assets are in dollars and Hong Kong dollars (55
percent USD/15 percent HKD)—a bit below the
dollar and Hong Kong dollar share of their liabilities
(swaps out of the HKD fund a decent share of the
banks euro assets).
Put simply, SAFE’s static dollar holdings aren’t the
important story.
The real story is the rapid growth of the state banks
dollar holdings.
Japan holds a high (though undisclosed) dollar share

in its FX reserves, and those reserves are primarily
invested in Treasuries—so Japan’s MoF is now clearly
the largest contributor to the U.S. data on foreign
official holdings of Treasuries. (SAFE has shifted its
funds out of U.S. custodians, and thus increasingly
appears in the data as a “private” holder in a European
custodial center)
Japan’s formal reserves haven’t changed much in the
last ten years; periodic sales to limit the yen’s weakness
have offset most of the MoF’s accumulated interest

income. Foreign currency reserves were $1.2 trillion
in 2014 and $1.16 trillion at the end of Q1. They
dipped to about $1 trillion at the end of August after
sales of close to $100 billion at the end of July/start of
August.

### Page 9

9
What has grown is the foreign portfolio of the GPIF.
it was around $400 billion in 2014, ~$1 trillion in Q1
of 2026.
GPIF’s foreign bonds aren’t held exclusively in dollars,
but roughly 52 percent of its foreign bonds (26
percent of all bonds) are USD-denominated.
But the GPIF, like most international investors, holds
a large share of its equity portfolio in the U.S. (~$165
billion, 65 percent of the foreign equity portfolio).
Korea is a more extreme version of Japan.

The Bank of Korea’s FX reserves have been roughly
$375-450 billion over the last ten years or so. Around
two-thirds of those are now in dollars (as of the end of
2025).

### Page 10

10
From 2012 to 2025, the foreign assets at the NPS
have grown from ~$80 billion (around 21 percent of
total assets) to over $660 billion (55 percent of total
assets)—and something like 70 percent of those are in
dollars. 76 percent of the fund’s equities are in dollars,
and 66 percent of its debt. And while a lower share of
its “alternative” portfolio is in North America, most
alternative mandates are awarded in dollars.

### Page 11

11
In other words, the majority of the dollar assets held
by Korea’s government (the NPS reports to the
Ministry of Health and Welfare, and the Minister of
Health chairs its asset allocation committee) aren’t
held by the central bank and aren’t in the IMF’s
COFER data set.
Taiwan is an unusual case, as the life insurers aren’t
technically sovereign investors. But with their
regulator now explicitly encouraging the them to hold
an unhedged foreign currency book and hence they

now rely on the state for their solvency, they have a bit
of an official character (a significant appreciation of
the TWD would blow through their volatility reserves
and leave them with fewer assets than liabilities, as
their foreign assets are largely held against TWD
policies). The CBC certainly now uses the lifers desire
to unwind existing hedges to help manage the Taiwan

### Page 12

12
dollar. And they have an exceptionally high dollar
share in their foreign assets—over 95 percent.
The Saudi Public Investment Fund (PIF) is another
example.
It keeps roughly $200 billion of its $250 billion in
foreign investments in dollars—well above the U.S.
share of a standard global stock index. Almost all of
the PIF’s $30 billion in external debt is also in dollars.
And the PIF now has only slightly fewer foreign
assets than the Saudi Central Bank has reserves.**

The broad story is thus pretty clear: the growth in the
world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign assets is not
coming through an increase in FX reserve holdings
managed by the world’s central banks.

### Page 13

13
And, in the critical case of China, the dollar share of
China’s quasi-sovereign foreign assets is likely higher
than the dollar share of their formal FX reserves.
Adam Tooze has noted that inflows into the U.S. are
now coming from investors looking for a profit, not
investors looking for safety. What he calls the “profit
dollar” manifests itself in a number of different ways.
More inflows into corporate bonds and equities for
one. Less official demand for Treasuries for another.

More opacity too.
Those same trends are at play among sovereign and
quasi-sovereign investors.
So don’t obsess about the dollar’s chare in formal FX
reserves. Do recognize that the dollar’s “reserve
currency role” isn’t the source of any significant new
inflows into the dollar.

### Page 14

14
The dollar’s dominant role in the international
monetary system depends on much more than the
size and composition of central bank FX reserves. It is
as much, perhaps more so (given that FX reserve
managers are themselves ultimately liability matchers)
a function of the portfolio choice of a set of
private/semi-private/quasi-sovereign investors that
are much more difficult to observe. Reserve currency
status is not only about FX reserves in a strict sense.

That’s been true for some time. Most of the current
flow into U.S. from the “official” sector is coming
from investors who are not classic reserve managers.
Those flows remain heavily tilted toward the dollar, at
least for now.***
And any real de-dollarization would likely occur first
among these investors.
Put differently, the dollar’s global role is increasingly
as a source of returns, not a source of safety. The
foreign bid, private and public, is for risk, not for

Treasuries. That doesn’t help Scott Bessent much
right now, but it has helped keep valuations in the
stock market extended. And the global debate on the
dollar’s role lags the evolution in the dollar’s role, and
the risks associated with that new role.
It is currently fashionable in some circles to point to
the diminution of the “convenience yield” on

### Page 15

15
Treasuries even while the U.S. dollar continues to
enjoy such a privilege. But the shift in the global
investor base, and their apparent portfolio preference
for U.S. risk assets in lieu of Treasuries, may help
explain the divergence between the convenience yield
on UST and the convenience yield on USD.
And of course, if the negative convenience yield
implies that Treasuries are trading at a historical
discount, the persistence of the latter—and its

concentration in risk assets—suggests that the “profit
dollar” itself is likely trading at a historical premium.
That apparent premium on U.S. risk assets deserves as
much (or more) scrutiny as the currency composition
of central bank FX reserves.
* The PIF and Saudi Aramco also now have a certain
amount of debt.
** Reserves (now mostly in Treasuries, so should
appear in the TIC data, unlike at some times in the
past.

### Page 16

16
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

CAPITAL FLOWS BANKING
*** Japan is the main exception; the GPIF’s dollar
share is below the likely dollar share of Japan’s MoF.

### Page 4

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Are investors rational?
The stock market strength and resilience over the last 3 months appears to be a rational response to strong
earnings and only limited signs that higher energy prices are materially affecting broad corporate profits.
Our dbLumina gauge indicates investors have been irrational during market

drops and periods of geopolitical tensions What does it mean to be ‘rational’?
Risk-off periods:
The tariff fears in 2025 and then the Iran
war in 2026 were key buying opportunities
in equity markets. Yet, the overwhelming
fear exhibited by investors in these periods
largely meant they sold off risk assets in an
emotional and irrational response.
Risk-on periods:
It can be rational to buy into a rising
market. Our indicator suggests this is what
happened in Q3 as earnings growth

continued to be strong.
Fear Indicators:
Sharp, disproportionate reactions to
threats, widespread sell-offs on
announcements, panic, flight to safety
beyond what might be fundamentally
warranted.
Investors
are less
rational
Investors
are more
rational

Luke.Templeman@db.com

Emotions cooled as ‘rational’ investing took over
Lower realised volatility in markets in Q3 helped clam emotional market responses. That indicates investors are
detaching from the volatile macro and geopolitical backdrop
Emotion dipped markedly in Q3 Month-by-month
July 2026: A fear-driven sell-off in

semiconductor stocks, particularly in
Korea, was balanced by optimism from
strong earnings reports from major tech
firms and a significant drop in oil prices.
August 2026: An easing in inflation
pressures lowered expectations of Fed
rate hikes and supported a rally in
Treasury markets. Strong corporate
earnings season and signs of de-
escalation in the Middle East
September 2026: Rising energy prices
and renewed uncertainty in the Middle
East fuelled a sell-off in global sovereign

bonds. Concerns about debt sustainability
and hawkish central bank commentary
weighed on equity markets despite some
encouraging economic signals.

### Page 9

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 1
A reallocation of global sovereign reserve assets is accelerating, spurred by
geopolitical uncertainty and the fear of trade and financial sanctions. Notably,
central banks are repatriating their physical assets, such as the Dutch and French

central banks which have recently moved gold reserves out of New York.
Furthermore, a World Gold Council survey reveals that 45% of global central banks
plan to increase their gold reserves over the next 12 months.
What the market underestimates: The market views the recent surges in gold prices
as temporary, cyclical safe-haven flows. In reality, sovereign institutions are
structurally preparing for a more fragmented global trade system where tariff

policies and sanctions may make dollar-clearing assets more difficult.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Interest rates: At a time when the "world is awash in debt", long-term interest rates
of some countries may be forced structurally higher to attract alternative private
capital.
Inflation: Unexpected dollar depreciation may increase the cost of imported goods
Equity Prices: High-growth US equity valuations, which are highly sensitive to the

long-term risk-free rate, may face multiple compression if bond yields are pushed
higher.
The market currently prices in a material, long-term GDP boost from AI-driven
productivity gains. However, some research from the IMF suggests that over-reliance
on standardised LLMs can produce a high degree of "sameness" and uniformity in
cognitive output. Furthermore, escalating intellectual property and copyright
litigation threaten to bifurcate the quality of AI models based strictly on developers'

licensing budgets.
What the market underestimates: Some investors assume that the exponential
curve of AI hardware capabilities translates directly into exponential economic
output. But there is a risk that if AI tools merely standardise corporate processes
rather than innovate them, and legal barriers restrict access to premium data, the
productivity boom may be less aggressive than expected.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
GDP growth: Structural growth ceiling if AI fails to deliver the multi-percentage

point boost to global productivity required to offset aging demographics.
Equity prices: A downward revaluation of the technology and software sector if
massive AI infrastructure expenditures fail to generate revenue according to existing
expectations.
Geopolitical fragmentation of global reserve assets The AI “sameness“ trap and the productivity stagnation1 2
8

### Page 10

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 2
Advanced economies are facing a dual demographic crisis: rapidly aging populations
and underfunded pension systems. This is forcing some older people to remain in or
re-enter the workforce out of financial necessity rather than choice.

What the market underestimates: Lower unemployment rates are celebrated as a
sign of economic strength. However, in structurally ageing societies like Japan and
parts of Europe, the growth of the labour force is increasingly driven by part-time,
older workers. Furthermore, G20 nations lack adequate, cohesive policy frameworks
to address these long-term demographic fiscal drains.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: An older workforce working out of necessity has less discretionary

spending power, potentially locking economies into persistent, structural demand-
side deflationary forces, offset by supply-side wage pressures in highly skilled
sectors.
GDP growth: Labour productivity growth that is lower than it might otherwise be as
the median age of the workforce climbs, limiting long-term potential GDP.
The geopolitical battleground is focussed on energy blockades (such as the Strait of
Hormuz) but underestimates the weaponisation of critical minerals. China currently

holds ‘escalation dominance’ over rare-earth mineral supply chains. The emerging
risk is the proactive restriction of these minerals to Western firms, such as recent
bans on supplying rare-earth inputs to some US firms.
What the market underestimates: The market treats rare-earth supply constraints as
temporary trade spat negotiation tactics. In reality, as Western nations aggressively
push for reshoring and friend-shoring, China is increasingly using its processing

monopoly as a long-term strategic blockade to try to slow Western high-tech,
defence and the energy transition.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: Upwards pressure on inflation as the cost of manufacturing electric
vehicles, semiconductors, and advanced electronics surges due to supply blockades
and the high capital cost of rebuilding localised processing facilities.
Interest rates: Central banks may be forced to keep policy rates higher to combat

the persistent supply-side inflation generated by supply-chain fragmentation.
The fragmentation of the EU’s political centre Critical mineral isolationism and ‘green’ supply chain blockades3 4
9

### Page 11

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 1
Our dbLumina AI model helps us identify the key emerging themes that have the potential to
most impact markets and economies in the short term
While the previous focus was on the AI hardware squeeze and
commercial competition, the narrative is shifting towards AI's role as a

critical component of national security and geopolitical leverage.
— AI access: We see this in President Trump's framing of AI development as a
geopolitical race ("whoever wins AI wins").
— AI as a bargaining chip: Several examples over the last 12 months including the
2025 deal whereby AMD agreed to pay 15% of sales from certain AI chips to the
US government in exchange for an export license. Even if these deals change, the
trajectory is one where access to AI technology is tied to national economic and

security interests.
— Direct military & security applications: Several examples including, cyberattacks
on critical infrastructure (such as a UK power plant), the use of AI in developing
"exceptional" phages to fight E.coli, and the growing field of AI security
discussions.
Outlook: Expect more government intervention, the use of AI technology access as a
tool in diplomatic negotiations, and a greater focus on the dual-use nature of AI in
both commercial and military contexts. This will likely create regulatory and

operational uncertainty for tech firms.
The trend of reshoring is evolving into a more proactive diplomatic
strategy where nations are forming new, often transactional, alliances
to navigate a world less dominated by a single superpower.
— Proactive alliance building: Canadian Prime Minister Carney has been front and
centre making his first international trip to France and the UK in an effort to
diversify away from traditional reliance on the US. That follows other key

meetings between Japan and India, and more.
— Bypassing traditional powers: Greenland signalled its willingness to turn to
China for investment if Western countries do not provide support, reinforcing the
leverage of some smaller nations.
— Shifting security arrangements: The US pushed for a ‘reassurance force’ in
Ukraine, while French President Macron hosted a "coalition of the willing" to
discuss Europe's defence without US guarantees. Further examples include the

discussion of a Franco-German "nuclear sharing" arrangement.
Outlook: The assumptions of a bipolar world order is giving way to a more complex,
multi-nodal system. This will create greater emphasis on regional blocs and issue-
specific coalitions. The stability of traditional alliances like NATO and the G7 remain
deeply uncertain.
AI’s impact on geopolitics and national security The fracturing of multilateralism and the rise of "Middle Power Countries" 1 2

### Page 12

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 2
These four key themes dbLumina identifies are sometimes ‘known’ by investors, however, they may be underestimated by the market,
particularly when there are some potentially series longer-term impacts
Previous talk of “sovereign fiscal fragility” has now manifested itself

into a severe market reaction. Although fears around government debt
and deficits pop up from time to time, the magnitude of September’s
bond sell-off has been compounded by oil price concerns
— Accelerating sovereign sell-off: Concerns about inflation and debt sustainability
are not new but the peaks in bond yields are. Yields on 10-year Treasuries, bunds,
and gilts all rose sharply, with bunds hitting their highest level since 2009.
— Fiscal policy as a direct market catalyst: The market is increasingly sensitive to

fiscal policy announcements. For example, in the UK postponed tax rises, led to a
spike in gilt yields. Similarly, France's political turmoil and concerns over its
budget deficit caused the Franco-German yield spread to widen significantly.
— The “awash in debt" narrative: Treasury Secretary Bessent's comment at the G20
that "The world is awash in debt" signalled that the issue is increasingly top-of-
mind for policymakers.
Outlook: Markets will be increasingly quick to punish any perceived fiscal

irresponsibility. Expect higher borrowing costs, currency volatility, and increased risk
of financial instability, especially for countries with high debt levels.
The strain in the US consumer and labour markets is now diverging
sharply from the health of large corporations, reinforcing the K-shaped
economy.
— Strong corporate performance: Earnings season for Q2 showed remarkably solid
global growth, and the resilience of large corporations was a recurring theme

that helped support equity markets despite other headwinds.
— Weak consumer & labour signals: Economic reports have showed some signs of
a weakening labour market, falling consumer confidence, and stress among low-
income Americans. The New York Fed's survey also showed households expect a
worse financial situation.
— Policy and market disconnect: While strong corporate performance depends on
a robust economy, the underlying consumer weakness is concerning. Expect the

market to continue its twitchy reaction to data, as investors struggle to reconcile
these conflicting signals.
Outlook: This theme is potentially the most consequential for the domestic US
economy. If corporate profits and equity markets "detach" from the real economy as
consumers perceive it, it could lead to significant social and political instability,
making economic policy even more difficult to navigate.
The return of global bond vigilantes The bifurcation of economies: corporate resilience vs. consumer strain3 4

## Document 394: “崩溃”！美国企业债市场“异常悲观”，高盛承认“或许还没到位”

Source PDF: all report pdf/“崩溃”！美国企业债市场“异常悲观”，高盛承认“或许还没到位”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI 资本⽀出浪潮正将全球债市推向临界点。 Meta ⾸现负⾃由现⾦流、 SpaceX 筹备 400 亿美
元芯⽚融资，⾼盛⾃⼰的 " 熊市基准 " 已被现实击穿 —— 信⽤策略师公开承认悲观情景 " 还不
够悲观 " 。与此同时，法国 10 年期国债收益率突破 5% ，创 2002 年来新⾼， AI 融资压⼒、年
末去杠杆与欧洲主权危机三重⻛暴骤然叠加，市场正在向全球最富有的科技巨头收取接近
垃圾级的融资溢价。
摘要：
美国企业债市场正经历⼀场由 AI 资本⽀出浪潮引发的深度压⼒测试，⽽⾼盛⾃⼰的信⽤策略师已
公开承认，此前设定的悲观情景或许还不够悲观。
上周， Meta 财报触发 AI 信⽤利差⼤幅⾛阔，其信⽤违约互换（ CDS ）创下历史新⾼，⾼盛旗下
AI 篮⼦⾃ 6 ⽉以来累计下跌 23% 。⾼盛对冲基⾦产品主管 Tony Pasquariello 在内部周报中直⾔，
其与信⽤市场资深⼈⼠的多次对话中，" 崩溃 " （ melting down ）⼀词被反复提及。
与此同时，⾼盛⾸席信⽤策略师 Amanda Lynam 在最新报告中承认，美国投资级（ IG ）和⾼收

益（ HY ）信⽤利差正快速逼近此前设定的下⾏情景峰值，并预计将 " 温和超出 " 该区间，⻛险 " 明
确偏向进⼀步⾛阔 " 。
这场动荡已从企业债市场蔓延⾄主权债券市场，并开始向欧洲主权债务领域延伸。法国 10 年期
国债与德国国债利差扩⼤⾄ 178 个基点，创 2011 年欧债危机以来最宽⽔平，法国 10 年期国债收
益率突破 5% ，为 2002 年以来⾸次。市场正⾯临 AI 融资压⼒、年末去杠杆情绪与欧洲主权⻛险三
重叠加的罕⻅局⾯。
AI 泡沫从企业债市场蔓延⾄债市
在过去⼤半年⾥，围绕 AI 的争论主要集中于股票估值 —— 市盈率倍数、资本⽀出指引，以及英伟
达的客⼾是否同时也是其投资者。这场争论正式演变为⼀个信⽤市场问题。
Meta 财报成为导⽕索。该公司公布了⾃ 2012 年 IPO 以来⾸个⾃由现⾦流为负的季度，其 CDS 随
即创下历史新⾼，股价⼤幅下挫。⾼盛投资级信⽤交易主管 Brad Shelofsky 将上周定性为 " 对 AI
⻛险的重⼤重新定价，以及市场对拥挤仓位的全⾯、激进去杠杆 " 。

Pasquariello 在其周报中点明了 AI 交易演变为债券市场问题的核⼼逻辑：需求端并⽆问题，⼤型
科技公司 " 继续保持良好执⾏ " ，但问题在于满⾜这⼀需求所需的⼀切 ——" 随着前瞻性⽀出承诺
持续累积，市场正在就必要原材料的供给提出⾮常尖锐的问题，其中最突出的两个变量是电⼒
和资本。 "
⾼盛利率交易员 Mitchell Cornell 描述了这⼀传导链条：利率上⾏、波动率上⾏的市场格局部分
由 "AI 资本⽀出发债 " 驱动，随后 " 部分⾼杠杆 AI 公司的信⼼开始动摇 " 。达拉斯联储主席 Lorie
Logan 表⽰，她估计政策利率⽬标区间还需再上调⾄少 50 个基点。廉价资⾦时代构建的 AI 资本⽀
出商业模式，正在遭遇货币环境的根本性挑战。
⾼盛悲观情景已被现实超越
⾼盛信⽤策略师 Amanda Lynam 此前为美国信⽤市场设定的下⾏情景峰值为：投资级利差 93 个
基点，⾼收益利差 335 个基点 —— 这是她的 " 熊市基准 " 。然⽽在最新报告中，⾼盛坦承这⼀预设

已不够悲观：
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

Lynam 列出了信⽤市场当前⾯临的三重压⼒：⼀是资本成本上升的考量；⼆是 AI 相关投资预期带
来的多年期融资浪潮；三是仓位与年初⾄今业绩动态，后者 " 可能正在加剧近期的 ' ⻛险规避 ' ⾛
势 " 。她认为，在现在⾄ 12 ⽉初之间，⻛险 " 明确偏向进⼀步⾛阔 " 。
尽管如此， Lynam 仍坚持认为 " 信⽤利差出现实质性、持续性⾛阔需要经济活动急剧下滑，⽽这
并⾮我们的基准情景 " ，并维持年末利差较当前⽔平 " 温和收窄 " 的⽬标 —— 理由是 12 ⽉供给放缓
通常带来的季节性收紧效应。然⽽， SpaceX 、 Meta 等超⼤规模科技公司正在筹备年内规模最⼤
的投资级债券交易，这⼀乐观预设⾯临严峻考验。
超⼤规模科技公司融资压⼒引爆市场
市场压⼒的两个核⼼来源，均指向融资⽽⾮营收。
SpaceX ⽅⾯，⻢斯克在财报电话会上表⽰，明年有 " 虽⼩但不为零 " 的可能性将超过 20GW 的超
级智能算⼒上线，并对增量约 10GW 的算⼒部署表达了⾼度信⼼。⽽ SpaceX 截⾄三季度末仅有

1.7GW 算⼒，计划年底达到 3GW 。市场 " ⾼度聚焦于每 GW 约 500 亿美元的成本 "—— 以 10GW 计
算，单⼀公司的资本⽀出规模将达 5000 亿美元。
随后， SpaceX 此前报道的 400 亿美元芯⽚融资计划（原定 2027 年）被披露将 " 可能于下周定
价 " 。受此影响， SpaceX 债券曲线⼤幅平坦化： SPCX 5.35% 2031 年债券当周利差⾛阔 55 个基
点⾄ T+225 ， 6.65% 2056 年债券⾛阔约 25 个基点⾄ T+285 。
Meta 则在其 14C ⽂件中留下了⼀段罕⻅的坦⽩，直指资本市场窗⼝正在收窄：" 特别委员会也认
识到，在竞争对⼿耗尽各类融资渠道之前抢先锁定资本，时机⾄关重要。近期其他知名公司已
完成⼤规模融资，且预计还有更多融资即将发⽣，以⽀持同⼀ AI 增⻓⽬标 —— 这已导致融资环境
⽇趋严峻。 "
英伟达 CEO ⻩仁勋对此的表述更为直⽩： " 资本需求远⼤于资本供给。这个问题终将得到解
决。 " 但正如 Shelofsky 所指出的，解决的⽅式将是价格 —— 即更⾼的利差。

债券市场遭遇 " 史上最差周 "
从具体数据来看，市场损伤触⽬惊⼼。
⾼盛投资级超⼤规模科技公司（ Hyperscaler ）篮⼦当周利差⾛阔 17 个基点， " 创 7 ⽉以来最差单
周表现 " ； 30 年期 Hyperscaler 篮⼦较 IG100 （剔除 Hyperscaler ）贵出 85 个基点，⾃该篮⼦ 2 ⽉成
⽴以来已累计⾛阔 90 个基点。换⾔之，市场正在向全球最⼤、最富有的科技公司收取接近垃圾
级的溢价，以为其 30 年期数据中⼼融资。
仓位层⾯的损伤同样惨烈。据⾼盛主经纪商数据，周四出现逾⼀年来最⼤规模的多头抛售，对
冲基⾦在过去⼋个交易⽇中有七天处于去杠杆状态；信息技术板块遭遇⾃ 2024 年 9 ⽉以来最⼤净
卖出。⾼盛对冲基⾦ VIP 配对篮⼦创 15 年来最差单周表现，五个交易⽇下跌 4.1% ；⾼动量篮⼦
年初⾄今涨幅全部回吐，回撤幅度达 43% 。⾼盛 TMT AI vs. AI ⻛险篮⼦周四单⽇下跌 10% ，为
DeepSeek 冲击以来最差单⽇表现，该篮⼦ 6 ⽉时年初⾄今涨幅⾼达 94% ，⽬前已转为年内下跌

13% 。
" 对于美元市场，我们此前将 2026 年 3 ⽉的利差峰值作为下⾏情景的基准（ IG 93 个基点， HY
335 个基点）。然⽽我们正在迅速逼近这些⽔平，现预计利差将温和超出上述区间。 "

### Page 3

⾼盛 EMEA 困境债务团队在周报中描述了更深层的市场裂痕： CCC 级利差当周⾛阔 55 ⾄ 65 个基
点，⾄ 1320 ⾄ 1340 个基点，⽉初⾄今下跌 3.5% ；全球投资级利差扩⼤⾄ 17 个⽉⾼位 129 个基
点；仅 10.1% 的贷款市场交易价格⾼于⾯值， 74.1% 处于 90 ⾄ 100 之间， " 对抗进⼀步利差⾛阔的
缓冲空间极为有限 " ；⼀级市场 " 基本关闭，机会性发⾏搁置，交易延迟，部分边际交易被撤
回 " 。
该团队在谈及 Virgin Media 债券持续下跌时，直接表⽰ " 我们收到了⼤量关于下跌原因的询问，
但此时此刻，除了技术性因素，我们已⽆法给出解释 " 。
欧洲主权⻛险⽕上浇油
就在 AI 融资压⼒尚未平息之际，欧洲主权债务市场再度成为新的压⼒源。
法国 10 年期国债与德国国债利差扩⼤⾄ 178 个基点，创 2011 年欧债危机以来最宽⽔平；法国 10
年期国债收益率突破 5% ，为 2002 年以来⾸次。法国银⾏次级债当周⾛阔约 25 ⾄ 30 个基点，欧洲

⾼收益信⽤指数 Xover ⾛阔 23.875 个基点。
⾼盛欧盟宏观信⽤团队注意到⼀个值得警惕的信号：信⽤表现开始跑输利率， " 感觉是很久以来
的第⼀次 " 。更令⼈担忧的是，在利差⾛阔过程中，通常会出售初级保护的卖⽅这次 " 根本没有出
现 " ，意味着市场正在向 " 离散度上⾏ " 的格局演变 —— 没有⼈愿意承保⻛险。
多空博弈：年末能否迎来反转？
⾯对这⼀局⾯，⾼盛内部的多空分歧依然存在，但措辞均趋于谨慎。
Pasquariello 承认上周市场 " 确实切到了⻣头 " ，周四科技敞⼝的抛售规模为过去两年之最，并
表⽰⾃⼰ " 正在舔舐伤⼝ " ， " 现在靠近岸边游泳没有任何问题 " 。但他同时指出，⾼盛仍预计
2027 年标普 500 每股盈利增⻓ 18% ， " 在两位数盈利增⻓的背景下，熊市极为罕⻅ " 。
Shelofsky 则坦承⾃⼰ " 过早陷⼊了均值回归的陷阱 " ，原本预期 Meta 发债将为四季度反弹创造空
间，但融资压⼒的担忧反⽽在上周进⼀步升温。他仍预计四季度某个时点将出现类似 7 ⽉的轧空

⾏情，但前提是 Meta 和 SpaceX 的芯⽚交易顺利完成，并警告年末情绪构成额外阻⼒： " 与 7 ⽉相
⽐，这⼀轮最棘⼿之处在于，市场在年末业绩承压的背景下普遍希望去杠杆 …… 参与者都在寻
求缩减规模、保住全年业绩、防御性地收官。 "
下周，市场将迎来 30 ⾄ 35 亿美元的投资级⼀级供给，以及⾕歌、亚⻢逊、微软财报和甲⻣⽂投
资者⽇， " 结果的区间极为宽泛 " 。

### Page 4

《消費量能未見失速訊號》
• 8月總體市場消費數據偏保守，
然維持正向表現
1
資產 4Q26 看法 投資展望
核心消費 中立 從已公告的核心消費個股展望，本行預期今年上下半年營收成長48：52
的溫和成長預期，同時零售商備貨行為與終端消費需求仍支持到貨量的
基本盤，但消費者實際支出意願可能受到油價／利率上升抑制，導致平
均單價、毛利率受到一定壓縮，尤其在低、中所得族群及易受油價影響
的旅遊／實體零售類別。4Q26預計呈「量能可望維持、獲利偏弱」格局
資料來源：美國銀行2026/9/15，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/18。

• 消費量能未見失速訊號 P.1
• 「K型」分化持續收斂，低所得消
費動能並非來自舉債 P.2
• 重塑消費環境與消費類股估值基礎 P.3
• 4Q26傳統銷售旺季，考驗獲利 P.4
消費產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/9/21
本行長期追蹤美國銀行信用卡消費統計數據(Consumer
checkpoimt，月報) 顯示，8月消費動能自7月疲弱中明顯
反彈。從美國銀行發行的信用卡、簽帳卡合計消費YoY成
長率已連續三個月降溫（6月6.3%→7月5.0%→8月4.5%）
剔除油品後同步走緩（5.6%→4.3%→3.7%），主因6月世
界盃與線上促銷時序錯位帶來的高基期效應消退。惟季調
後MoM動能出現反轉，7月一度下滑（-0.2%），8月明顯
回升（+0.9%）。商務部數據同步佐證：8月零售銷售季調
後月增1.2%，優於市場預期0.7%，成長動能來自線上零售、
加油站（價格推升）及餐飲、電子產品等類別。
